# Matic Desk — Decision Register

**Version:** 2.34 — the single register. **This file owns the numbering.** (2.34, 2026-09-10, last: **D249 — this environment's own identifiers are SHOWN and never SAID: the machine's name, the signed-in account, and this machine's own addresses.** Charles: *"Speaking computer names could be tricky especially in a corp environment. Ok to display computername but it should say this pc"*, then *"do the username too"*, then *"fix 2 and 3 as well"*. Each carries a department, a site, a person or a subnet; each is read out in an open-plan office; and each is bad audio. **Nothing spoke the name or the account, and that was an accident of three unrelated decisions rather than a guarantee** — the design's own placeholder consent card reads *"…on PLACEHOLDER-PC"*, which is the sentence it would arrive through. **The address was different: it WAS spoken**, by `renew_dhcp`'s success line. `say()` is the only thing that reaches the voice, so it renders one string and speaks another; the rule is in a pure `spoken-form.js` because `renderer.js` cannot be imported by a headless test. **The boundaries are the interesting part and every one is wrong in a way reading does not show:** a hyphen is a word boundary to a regex, so `OLD-FIN-LON-0442` would have matched; `CORP\a.petrova` without a qualifier clause becomes `CORP\your account`, the account redacted and the domain read out; an address cannot exclude `.` on its right because sentences end; and a machine called `PC` would have rewritten the word "pc" in every sentence. **§5.32a: the addresses are this machine's two literal values, not a pattern — a hub answer may legitimately say "try 8.8.8.8", and redacting that destroys the answer.** §5.32c fixes the third item, the panel's crash note, which put a JavaScript exception at the user. §5.32d records that the tests were mutation-probed, all ten weakenings caught — **and that the probe itself was wrong twice first, both times reporting the shape of a real finding while proving nothing.** **1098 / 17 client assertions.** §5.32. 2.33, 2026-09-10, last: **D247 — the broker's strings, audited, and the D246 split applied one layer down.** §5.30's ledger said the broker-derived strings were "likelier to be fine, but that is not checked". They were not fine: `ExecutionResult.message` is SPOKEN on every path and carried `OpenServiceW(spooler) failed (Win32 error 5)`, the consent protocol's own vocabulary ("no consent nonce was presented"), and the SERVICE'S REGISTRY KEY — *"Restarted the spooler service"*, to someone whose printer had stopped and who cannot find that name anywhere in Windows. The catalog carries `plain.serviceNames` now (what `services.msc` calls it), the consent card's rollback note names a capability by its title rather than its id, and the renderer no longer interpolates `validation.errors[0]` or `prepared.error` into `say()` at all. **Nothing was deleted** — every diagnostic is on `detail` and in the audit record. **D248 is PROPOSED and awaits Charles:** the consent card's `restart_service(serviceName=spooler)` is a documented, screen-verified disclosure, and D244's answer (gloss it, keep the exact call subordinate) needs a contract change — so it was STYLED subordinate, not rewritten. §5.31b: **the check page returned success and only success**, so none of the three failed-fix paths had ever been rendered; driving them found a duplication introduced in this same pass. **1048 / 16 client assertions.** §5.31. 2.32, 2026-09-10, later still: **D246 — a failed egress returns TWO strings: `error`, which the user is told and which is persona data, and `detail`, which is ours and goes to the inspector, the developer log and the egress record.** `HubClient.fileTicket()` had one field doing both jobs and the renderer SPOKE it, so a user whose ticket had just failed heard *"Could not reach the hub at http://127.0.0.1:8787: fetch failed"* — our word, our port and Node's phrasing, read aloud. All five failure paths were ours, including a bare `HTTP 503`. **Nothing was deleted:** every diagnostic moved to `detail`. §5.30a: **"the hub" was also on the review card and in the tickets band**, which are read far more often than any failure — the card is the one screen where a user approves data leaving the machine, and it named the destination in our word. Both say **the service desk** now, with every fact intact; the assertions that broke were pinning phrasing and now pin facts. **"In this build" was left alone on purpose** — `persona.tickets.some` says it in the same words and D242 requires the band and the spoken answer to agree. §5.30c: **the failure branch had never been run** (it needs a hub up enough to offer and down enough to refuse), so the check page has two one-shot triggers now and both were driven. **1012 / 16 client assertions.** §5.30. 2.31, 2026-09-10, late: **D244–D245 — a closed enum that reaches the user is GLOSSED and not hidden, and a fact about what the system did not do is said once, on the surface shown before the user acts.** From a screenshot of a running session: the review card printed `CATEGORY needs_human_judgement` at an office worker, and the no-email fact appeared twice three lines apart in two wordings. Hiding the enum was the wrong fix — D128 requires the user to see what is being sent — so it is glossed with the raw value kept underneath, from a table the enums in `prompt.js` are the authority for. **Two of the handover's four observations were already answered in code and the handover said the code wins:** the unreachable transition has announced itself in the conversation since D240, but it named only the service desk where the other two surfaces name the company's guidance as well, and it promised that *questions and tickets will wait* when nothing is queued at all. The idle status is now "Nothing in progress" — "Nothing running", beside a band listing this machine's services, reads as a claim about them. §5.29b records four items for the next design delivery and answers two of them: the disclosure square **is not a checkbox** (both triggers are `<button>`, the square is a decorative span) and the label column is **3.53:1 in light, 4.65:1 in dark** — arithmetic over declared tokens, not a measurement. Found by clicking rather than reading: the disclosure chevron never rotated (`63011a4` re-keyed the open trigger's wash off `data-set` and missed it), and the check page's own fixture carried a category that is not in the enum. **923 / 16 client assertions.** §5.29. 2.30, 2026-09-10, evening: **D240–D243 — the end-user panel is Claude Design's delivery, and the endpoint's own machinery moves to an inspector that is hidden by default.** The state name, the voice pill, the facts table and four JSON panels are gone from the surface; what stayed is every line about what the system did NOT do, because each of those exists because of a shipped defect (D86, D205, D213, `700d920`). The stylesheet is GENERATED from the delivery and checked (D241, the avatar's argument again) — so a design change arrives as a new delivery — and the check page's frame is generated from `index.html` for the same reason. **D242 ships the tickets band the brief did not ask for, reading the endpoint's own egress record, with "Progress not visible" where a status would go — it is PROPOSED and awaits Charles.** D243 persists the theme and stops the window being shown before it has painted. **869 / 16 client assertions.** Nobody has watched the panel in Electron yet: `client/docs/PANEL-RENDERER-CHECK.md`. §5.28. 2.29, 2026-09-10, same morning: **D235 — "where's my ticket?" is answered from the endpoint's own egress record, and the capability answer is generated from a new catalog field, `plain.helpsWith`, in the user's words.** Charles asked for his open tickets' status and the knowledge base declined it as a gap; the endpoint now recognises the question, lists what THIS machine filed (reference, when, the user's own words) and says plainly that checking a ticket's progress is not built — the hub-side lookup needs a route and a user identity (D54, D68/D69). He also asked for a friendlier capability summary as tools grow: the list is still generated (D165), but from "what you have" phrases in the catalog rather than titles — a capability without one is read by its title, which is the honest fallback. **797 / 15 client assertions.** §5.27. 2.28, 2026-09-10, morning after: **D234 — the assistant has a persona file.** Charles's first two runs of the new avatar: "hello matic how are you?" and "Hello Matic How are you today?" both left the machine (the residue test saw "matic", then "today", as a one-word complaint), "what can you fix?" was not a capability question, the fallback said "I could not reach the model" over a 502 with a green pill beside it, the app threw "Object has been destroyed" on close, and the verdict was *"I am not feeling the love."* `client/src/router/persona.json` now holds the name, the vocatives, the boot line, every social line (rotated, warm, plain) and the two last-resort sentences; nothing in it may name a capability or claim an outcome, and `test/persona.test.mjs` checks both against the catalog's own words. The social markers and filler are widened; the name is stripped as a vocative. **746 / 14 client assertions.** Also found on that run: the hub was answering with `embedder: none` (lexical leg only, so "shortcut to hide desktop" retrieved 0 chunks and declined) while Ollama had the embed model — a hub started degraded stays degraded; restart it through the launcher. §5.27. 2.27, 2026-09-10, later: **D232 — the avatar is Claude Design's v7 bust chassis, driven from its manifest, and a knowledge-base decline now has a face (`declining`); D233 — the endpoint has a connectivity model (D49's three states, D51's hysteresis) and the reconnect question is answered: endpoints come to the hub, the hub signals nobody, the heartbeat response is the directive channel.** The audit the avatar handover demanded found the old avatar had no `declining` and rendered a decline as `thinking → speaking → idle` with a red system line; three other state misuses against v7's placement rules were corrected (a failed fix was `error`, an offer was `escalating`, ACCESS_DENIED was `escalating`). **§3's phoneme risk was measured: 732 real Piper phonemes, and the manifest's eSpeak-ASCII table knows none of the IPA Piper emits (58% of occurrences would close the mouth), so the debugged table stays — and the same run found five bare vowels that table had never matched, because length marks arrive as their own rows.** Also found: the delivered harness's dark-panel toggle does not knock the field out (a token declared on the svg beats one inherited from a wrapper), and its reduced-motion toggle does not reach the tray strip. **650 / 13 client assertions, up from 492 / 11.** Nobody has watched the Electron window with the new face. §5.27. 2.26, 2026-09-10, night: **D231 — B3's enrollment mechanism is built (pending queue, auto-approve + per-tenant toggle, rotatable time-boxed secret, rate limiting with a volume-based fallback to the queue, bulk revocation by batch), and device identity (D60/D61/D63/D64/D146/D148 — the TPM-backed key, the broker-custody chain, the token exchange) is deliberately NOT**, because that whole family is one dependency chain and no link of it means anything built alone: D61 needs a key to sign with, and D64's broker custody needs the named-pipe transport, which is designed and not built (D200/D201). `submitEnrollment()` in `server/src/devices/enrollment.js` is the one function the console, the CLI (`bin/devices.mjs`) and `POST /v1/enroll` all compose (D224's pattern, a third time). A device on the Agents page is KNOWN, not PROVEN, and the page says so. **384 / 342 assertions, up from 378 / 310.** §5.26. 2.25, 2026-09-09, night: **D230 DECIDED — the article editor is a structured form, not a markdown editor.** Charles confirmed the handover's recommendation ("option 1"): fields for `problem`/`symptoms[]`/`keywords[]`/`guidance[]`/`escalateWhen[]`/`doNotAttempt[]`/`cause`, one entry per line on the arrays, no markdown, no sanitiser, no new dependency — `raw()` stays unused. `body` (markdown, `type: "reference"` only) stays uneditable and is shown back unchanged. Built the same session: `GET /admin/knowledge/new`, `GET /admin/knowledge/:id/edit`, `POST /admin/knowledge/save`, all writing through `ingestAndEmbed()` (D228) like the paste-a-bundle form; every save lands `lifecycle = 'unvalidated'`; `kb.article.write`/`.refused` audits the id and the changed field names, never text; an id collision on create is refused (409) rather than silently overwritten; an edit cannot rename the id or blank `body`/`scopeNote`/`provenance`. **378 / 310 assertions, up from 366 / 268.** **Not done: the model's-answer half of "preview before publish"** — retrieval-only preview is built by reuse (the existing search page); the completion provider is not threaded into `adminHandler()`, only the embedder is, and that wiring is a decision this row does not take. §5.25. 2.24, 2026-09-09, evening: **D229 RATIFIED as recommended — logged question text is kept 90 days then cleared, the row stays, the sweep runs inside every write, configurable downward only — and D227 FOLLOWS: the demo and dev hubs log question text from now on.** Nothing is retroactive. 2.23, 2026-09-09, later still, and BOTH FOUNDER QUESTIONS ANSWERED THE SAME DAY: **D226 is DECIDED, NARROWER THAN IT WAS ASKED** — the console shows a matched chunk's text **for the chunks `/v1/ask` would send and no others**, which is the text that produced or failed to produce an answer and the smallest disclosure that answers the question the screen exists to answer; the narrowing is structural (`sentText` is null below the top 5 and the legs are stripped of text before they leave the module), so a page cannot leak an unsent chunk by forgetting a check. **D227 is DEFERRED behind a new D229** — a retention policy for logged question text, on the founder's reasoning that a table of what employees typed needs a keeping rule *before* it has rows in it. So `MATIC_KB_LOG_QUERIES` stays off, the review queue keeps showing declines without their text, and **that cost is named rather than absorbed**. **D229 is the cheapest item on the list to answer, because nothing has been logged yet.** 2.22, 2026-09-09, later still: **D226–D228 in §5.24 — Knowledge is real in the console: a bundle can be ingested from the browser and the corpus searched the way `/v1/ask` searches it,** with both retrieval legs shown unfused beside the fusion and the D207/D203/§5.16c caveats on the screen. **D228 (done) makes the console and the CLI share ONE ingest** — `ingestAndEmbed()`, which refuses the embed step rather than put a second embedding model in the corpus, so exit criterion 1 ("the page's counts equal what `kb:status` prints") is a real test. **D226 and D227 are PROPOSED and await the founder:** whether the matched chunk's TEXT may be shown on a hub-rendered console page (D53 was written before the console existed), and whether `MATIC_KB_LOG_QUERIES=1` should be set on the dev and demo hubs — the one switch that turns the retrieval log into a table of what employees typed. Neither blocked building; the console ships with chunk text absent and logging off. §5.24 also records four defects found by RUNNING the ingest rather than reading it, the sharpest being that **migration 002 has no CHECK on `platform`, so an article stamped `Windows 11` is stored, indexed, embedded and retrieved by nothing** — legal, invisible, and now reported at ingest as a warning. Also `bin/admin.mjs reset`, so a lost authenticator secret is a reset rather than a new account. 2.21, 2026-09-09, later: **D225 — the console wears the Industry design system**, applied from Claude Design's handback the same day; the three Barlow faces self-hosted from the hub. 2.20, 2026-09-09: **D221–D224 in §5.23 — the admin console scaffold, B2.** Server-rendered HTML at `/admin` in the hub process, no new dependency; local accounts with scrypt + mandatory TOTP, roles carrying permissions through a new `admin_role_permissions` table, admin-action audit from the first request; the review queue is home (D177) with the D205 declines as its one live feed; tickets fully working through a query layer the CLI shares; five honest stubs. **D223 records the founder's 2026-09-09 answer that a declined question is a gap record and not a D154 transcript.** Eight exit criteria pass on PostgreSQL 17: `server/docs/B2-ADMIN-CONSOLE.md`. Nobody has clicked through it in a browser yet. 2.19, 2026-09-05: **D220 in §5.22 — the demo-prep pass.** Two Desktop launchers that preflight before they start (Docker, Postgres, migrations, a tenant, THE CORPUS CHUNK COUNT, Ollama, exact model tags, VRAM, the port) and print READY only after both models are resident and one question and one proposal have gone through the hub; `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=24h` set and verified on `ollama ps`. **§5.22a explains the 90-second cold load: it is a SHARED GPU.** Measured 12.7–14.5 s with the card free, 190 s load / 265 s wall with a game holding ~7 GB, and the model then split 15/85 CPU/GPU. Warm turns are 3–8 s. §5.22b records what was found and left: a declined question offers a ticket the question path cannot open. 2.18, 2026-09-04: **D219 in §5.21 — THE STATE FALLBACK STOPS ANSWERING QUESTIONS NOBODY ASKED.** §5.20b listed it as live and unfixed hours earlier. `routeByState` ignored the text by its own design, so on a machine with a stopped print spooler *"the screen on my laptop is cracked"* came back as *"the print spooler service is stopped, I can start it again."* **The question was never "should it read the text" but "does the text entitle it to ignore it"** — ignoring it is CORRECT for *"help"* with the cable out, which is the Day-4 exit criterion. `stateTopicsFor()` answers that: a named symptom restricts which branches may reply, a sentence with no subject lets any of them, and a sentence naming something else lets none. **The design had met this failure mode once already and half-solved it** — `flush_dns`’s `egressFailed` gate asks whether an observation SUPPORTS the proposal; nothing asked whether it was about the COMPLAINT, and a stopped spooler is a good observation about the wrong thing. §5.21a states the cost of the whitelist bias plainly, and §5.21b records that the caller’s last-resort reply had to change too, because it claimed the machine was clean in a state where that is false — replacing a non-sequitur with an untruth would not have been a fix. 2.17, 2026-09-04: **D213–D218 in §5.20 — ESCALATION NOW PRODUCES A TICKET, and the avatar stopped talking about the user in the third person.** Both defects came from the FIRST CLICK-THROUGH of hub mode. **D214 is the one nobody had written down:** `escalationSchema()` asks for `summary` as a third-person case note for the assignee — correctly — and all three providers spliced it into the spoken reply, so the avatar said *"The user reports a cracked screen."* One string, two audiences; the model was obeying its instructions exactly, and the schema was wrong. The spoken line is now generated client-side by `escalationReply()`, never contains `summary`, and **is an offer rather than a report** — *"I would hand this to X"* is reported speech with nothing to answer, which is why the observed *"yes please hand to it"* landed nowhere. **D213 is B6-lite:** `migrations/tenant/003-tickets.sql`, `POST /v1/ticket`, `bin/tickets.mjs`, and **NO OUTBOUND EMAIL**, so per D86 it is a record and not a queue — a fact repeated on five separate surfaces so that claiming otherwise requires ignoring a value rather than not knowing one. **D215** keeps the profile locally per user and the list on the hub, self-asserted and labelled so everywhere, with `osUsername` read live rather than stored. **D216** makes the interview a declarative field list that asks only what it cannot derive and **NEVER TRAPS A TURN** — an unrecognised answer to the offer passes through and is handled as a new problem. **D217** gives egress its own log recording the payload verbatim, because filing is the first time identity leaves the endpoint and `Agent.identify()`'s *"Nothing here is sent anywhere"* is no longer unconditionally true. **D218 was found by the end-to-end run and then reproduced deliberately:** `HubClient` gave up on the hub 3.5 minutes before the hub gave up on its model, and `routeByState()` answered a cracked screen with the print spooler's state. **§5.20b lists what is still broken, including that fallback, which is NOT fixed.** 2.16, 2026-09-04: **D212 in §5.19 — D165'S SOCIAL LAYER IS BUILT, and D198's defect is fixed.** *"Hello how are you?"* no longer produces a consent card. Six deterministic client-shipped subkinds, and **the capability answer is GENERATED from `broker.listCapabilities()`** — D165's actual requirement, because a hand-written list is a claim and a generated one is a fact; with an unreadable catalog it promises nothing at all. **`Agent.propose()` calls it UNCONDITIONALLY, which corrects D211's gating:** D198's defect was observed on `provider: "cloud"`, the frozen demo's configuration, so gating this on the hub would have left the defect on the path it was seen on. **§5.19a records that the hard part was not recognising "hello" but not swallowing "hi, my printer is broken"** — a silent failure worse than D198's — plus two defects found by running it: a bare "Hi" classified as an action (punctuation enumerated instead of word boundaries), and "what is the weather" sent to the knowledge base (off-topic markers were taking the residue test, and bare topic nouns collide with real complaints). D165's row no longer says "not built". 2.15, 2026-09-04: **D211 in §5.18 — THE ENDPOINT CAN ASK A QUESTION.** B7a built a hub that answers and left nothing able to ask it; `client/src/router/classify.js` splits question from action after tier-0, `HubClient.ask()` calls `/v1/ask`, and an answer comes back as `res.answer` with the citation kept OUT of the spoken reply (H5). Three load-bearing properties: the classifier is **biased towards the measured path** (an action verb beats question grammar, so "how do I fix my printer" proposes); **a declined question does NOT fall through** to the proposal path, because that would raise the unprovoked consent card D198 forbids; and the branch is gated **by capability, not provider name**, so the frozen demo cannot reach it by construction. **§5.18a records the integration bug only an end-to-end run could find:** `probeMachine()` has no `platform`, so every Windows-only article was invisible to the endpoint — both sides' tests passed and the seam between them was broken. **§5.18b records that porting the test runner to `client/` found TWO test files that had fallen out of `npm test`** — one costs money, one needs elevation, both belonged out, and neither absence was recorded; exclusions are now declared with a reason and printed every run. **D165's social layer is still NOT built** and a test asserts D198's greeting still routes to the proposal path, so the defect is recorded rather than masked. 2.14, 2026-09-04: **Q35 ANSWERED BY THE FOUNDER — English only, FOR NOW — and the code-signing certificate has its first estimate: roughly one month, ≈2026-10-04.** §5.17, D208–D210. **D208** records the answer and that *"for now"* is part of it: it licenses choosing English-only components where they are cheaper, and does NOT license making a reversal a migration rather than a redeploy. **D209 selects within D207's own recorded fork** — the reranker pick becomes `gte-reranker-modernbert-base` (150M, int8 ONNX, 8192 context) over `gte-multilingual-reranker-base` (306M, no ONNX), which matters more than Q35 does for the embedder because a cross-encoder's cost is per-candidate, so halving parameters halves every question. **D210 is the uncomfortable one: D199's deciding reason was removing Q35 from the critical path, and that reason is now SPENT.** D199 stands, `nomic-embed-text-v1.5` (137M, same 768 dims) is the pre-argued alternative, and it is NOT adopted today because no latency measurement exists for either and *"for now"* can revert. §5.17a records what the answer does not change — B7a needs no re-embed. **§5.17 also records that Q35, Q9 and Q39 are cited throughout this register and defined nowhere in it**, their text being in the workbook that has never been on disk. OPEN-9 and §9 carry the certificate estimate and its three consequences, the sharpest being that **Q9 — not the certificate — is now the item on the critical path.** 2.13, 2026-09-04: **D203–D207 in §5.16, from building B7a — three of them contradict a premise of the B7a spec.** **D203: D199's embedder is NOT in Ollama's library**; the dev embedder is the L variant (1024 dims, measured), which is 1.9× the parameters and MTEB-ranked *better* — so dev latency is a pessimistic proxy and dev quality an optimistic one, and the L sits in the parameter class D199 rejected. Not an amendment to D199. **D205: nothing in this system could return a prose answer** — `tool_choice: "required"` on both providers, HTTP 502 on a no-tool-call from `/v1/turn` — so an `answer` tool carries the reply and makes the decline a testable field. **D206: questions go to a second route `/v1/ask`**, because adding a database to `/v1/turn` would have meant deleting the test that keeps it stateless. **D204: the generic corpus lives in the control plane.** **D207: the reranker is retrieved and deliberately not picked** — TEI supports only four reranker types, and a cross-encoder costs per-query × per-candidate rather than per-query, which is the question and is nowhere in the handovers. §5.16a records that the relevance floor is still unset on purpose, and §5.16b a second instance of §10.5's trap class: `"darwin".includes("win")` is true. 2.12, 2026-09-04: **D152–D160 (OPEN-6) RATIFIED AS A SET by the founder**, as a separate act from B1's ratification the same day — §5.7 keeps the finding that prompted it, that B1's ratified schema had already implemented these shapes and was never evidence they were accepted. **D158 is ratified but inert in Phase 1** and B2 must not build an approval workflow against it. **D202 is proposed and awaits the founder** (§5.7a): step-up for vendor-grant creation, the one item D157's list missed. **§5.7b adds the migration provenance convention** — a migration names the decision it implements and the status that decision held when the migration was written — applied retroactively to `001-control-plane.sql`. 2.11, 2026-09-04: **D167–D192 and D193–D197 RATIFIED AS SETS by the founder**, so the KB architecture and the B1 control plane are settled. §5.11c keeps three caveats that ratification does not soften (D173's premise was not confirmed against KCS; D174's reranker is unretrieved; nothing is measured on our corpus). §5.12 records a correction to this file's own bookkeeping: those five cells always read *decided*, while the indexes called them *proposed*. **D152–D160 (OPEN-6) remain proposed** and §5.11c's old "ratify alongside OPEN-6" pairing is void as guidance. 2.10, 2026-09-04: **D199, D199a, D200 and D201 RATIFIED by the founder.** The embedder is decided, the pipe protocol's wire format is now a contract rather than a proposal, and D201's exception to D98 is accepted — which commits us to listing the new pipe-lifecycle calls in `BROKER-SURFACE.md` before any implementation merges. D193–D197 (B1) and D167–D192 (KB architecture) remain **proposed** and still need ratifying as sets. 2.9, 2026-09-04: **D201's blocker is measured, not reasoned — §10.5.** The DACL Windows gives a Node pipe was read off a live one, and the access check refused `GENERIC_WRITE` with ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED from an unelevated token. Reproducible: `npm.cmd run verify:pipe` from `client/`. 2.8, 2026-09-04: **D200–D201 in §5.15 — the named-pipe protocol, designed.** D200 is the wire shape (JSON Lines, the four Broker methods, D133's single strict version, `execute` with nowhere to put a proposal). **D201 is a forcing discovery: the transport we assumed cannot work** — libuv passes NULL security attributes to `CreateNamedPipeW`, so a LocalSystem-hosted Node pipe grants the unprivileged agent read and no write. It needs native pipe creation, which is a costed exception to D98. §5.15a records that a pipe DACL is not a defence against A1 and cannot be. 2.7, 2026-09-04: **D199 in §5.14 — the embedder is `snowflake-arctic-embed-m-v2.0`, proposed**, resolving the last of the Phase 1 plan's two "blocking now" items and unblocking B7; **D199a records voyage-4-nano as open-weight now and the one to revisit if its shared-embedding-space claim is verified.** §5.14a notes that both "blocking now" items were already resolvable — the embedder's gating `[VERIFY]` had been confirmed in §10.4 the day before, so the decision was waiting on nothing. 2.6, night of 2026-09-03: **D198 in §5.13** — an unrequested consent card is a cost to D130, so D165's social layer is a safety requirement and must sit ahead of the state fallback; found by using the app, not by reading it. §10.3a records the D130 renderer check, verified on a screen. 2.5, night of 2026-09-03: B1's six exit criteria run and pass — 56 assertions on PostgreSQL 17.11 — so D193's extension arrangement and **D195's limit** are confirmed against a live server rather than reasoned; ## 0. Provenance, numbering and gaps

**D1–D41 are not in this file.** They live in `ASK-IT-questionnaire.xlsx`, which was never downloaded: it exists only as a chat artifact in the conversation *3D avatar with Amazon Bedrock architecture*. Section 1 below reserves the range and records what is known about it. Reconstructing D1–D41 into this file is the highest-value housekeeping task remaining, because H1–H3e are defined there and several decisions below reference that range.

**The +7 renumbering has been applied, once, here.** The two earlier decision files and the consolidated register numbered from D35 and collided with D35–D41 in the workbook. Per the D91–D124 document's §0, every ID in the consolidated register — headings, table cells and cross-references — has been shifted by +7 in this file. Nothing outside this file should be renumbered again; cite this file.

**D130–D150 were assigned in this file on 2026-09-03.** They are the decisions the independent design review (`sources/2026-09-02-fable-design-review.md`) proposed or forced. The review itself did not number them. Two anchors were fixed by the founder's task list before the range was filled: **D130** is the consent-surface finding (review §1.1) and **D139** is the C9 documentation correction. The remaining nineteen were ordered by the review's own priority. **Every D130–D150 entry is marked with a status**; "proposed" means the founder has not ratified it, and the review's argument is cited so it can be rejected on its merits.

**D130–D168 were briefly double-assigned on 2026-09-03, and this file's assignment is authoritative.** Two documents written in a chat session running in parallel with this consolidation — `2026-09-03-phase0-decisions-D130-D150.md` and `2026-09-03-kb-architecture-authoring-ux.md` — numbered their own content from D130 (the first as D130–D150, the second as D151–D168). Neither was on disk when this note was written; the founder's handover `2026-09-03-claude-code-handover-register-merge.md` (in `Downloads/`) carries a summary of the first and three corrections from the second, and §5.6–§5.9 below were merged from that summary. A reader who finds either document should treat every ID in it as void: §5.6 maps the first document's IDs onto this file, and its genuinely new content is D151–D160. **The second document was found on 2026-09-03 (night)** and is filed at `../kb/2026-09-03-kb-architecture-authoring-ux.md` — not in `sources/`, because it is the live KB architecture document rather than superseded material, and its §3 was rewritten against retrieved sources when it was filed. It carries a banner stating that its own IDs are void. **Its content is merged as D167–D192 in §5.11**, which supersedes §5.9. The first document (`2026-09-03-phase0-decisions-D130-D150.md`) is still not on disk; when found it goes into `sources/` verbatim and is not cited.

**Verification status.** Every vendor claim in D42–D129 was written without web access and carries `[VERIFY]`. The 2026-09-02 review researched most of them against primary sources; §10 maps each item to its answer. Where a claim below still says `[VERIFY]`, it is still unverified.

**No endpoint performance number has been measured.** Four measurements now exist in the project (the fourth, 2026-09-03 night: the 40-case bake-off through the B0 hub reproduces the direct path case for case — §10.3): the model bake-off (one machine, 40 hand-written cases), the prompt-prefix token count (D140) and a live prompt-cache verification on `claude-sonnet-5` (D161). Everything else is a target.

**All §2 constraints in the hub handover are single-source** from one design-partner meeting.

## 2. Invariants

| ID | Rule | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **H1** | The playbook signing key never lives on the hub | Locked (D1–D41) | Review B1/H1: the rule must also cover *authority to cause a signature* — a hub that can drive a signing service is equivalent to a hub with the key (see C3, D134). |
| **H2** | The hub distributes policy; the endpoint enforces it | Locked (D1–D41) | Direction added by H4. |
| **H3** | No cloud credentials on the endpoint in joined mode (standalone is a bounded exception capped at R1) | Locked (D1–D41) | Device key is broker-side (D64), which is what makes H3 hold for the hub credential. |
| **H3b** | Playbook parameters are schema-constrained, never freeform | Locked | Review: the escalation payload is freeform model text that reaches a privileged human via the ticket path; H3b's scope is the executor. Recorded, not changed. |
| **H3c** | `toolConfig` is a schema-enforcement device, never an executor binding | Locked | Review: `whenNotToUse` is catalog prose fed to the model — the catalog review checklist must include "read the model-facing prose as a prompt". |
| **H3d** | Playbooks are typed primitives, never scripts | Locked | Review: tier must become a property of the playbook, not only of each primitive (three R1 steps can exceed one R2). |
| **H3e** | The model never authors the text a user consents to (the endpoint owns consent wording) | Locked | **Holds and is insufficient**: nothing says which *process* displays the text. See D130 — demonstrated 2026-09-03. |
| **H4** | Hub-supplied policy may only **narrow** endpoint capability, never widen it. Maximum risk tier and primitive allowlist are pinned at enrollment or carried in signed content. | **Proposed — not ratified** | Ratify with the amendments in D134: tier labels and consent text are part of the signed floor; the vendor catalog is the floor. Until ratified, C6 stands. |
| **H5** | Hub-supplied text that reaches the user is inert and attributed. Plain text only — no links, no HTML, no actions. Rendered in chrome visually distinct from the agent's own voice, attributed to IT, never spoken by the avatar as its own words. | **Proposed** | Second clause needed (D136): hub text entering the *model's* context is wrapped as data and never enters the system prompt. Shipped code speaks the escalation summary in the avatar's voice — a UX change H5 forces (X-5). |
| **H6** | Signed content carries a freshness bound (not-valid-after or minimum-acceptable-version) so an endpoint can detect staleness the hub never advertises. | **Proposed** | Detects but does not decide (D135): on expiry drop to the vendor floor catalog — never refuse (fleet outage) or merely warn (nothing prevented). Only not-valid-after can detect silence; a version floor cannot. |

**Why H4.** H1 stops a compromised hub signing new playbooks. H2 does not stop a compromised hub *widening the approved subset or raising the tier ceiling* using only correctly-signed content. H2 as worded is satisfied by an endpoint faithfully enforcing a policy telling it to do more. The invariant needs the *direction* stated.

**Why H5.** Announcements are hub-supplied text rendered inside a trusted taskbar agent — content, not policy, so H2 and H4 do not reach it. A compromised hub could push *"IT needs you to re-enter your password here"* to every endpoint without violating any invariant. The same gap exists in shipped code: escalations are not re-validated by the broker and the transcript speaks the owner aloud. H5 is H3e one layer out.

**Why H6.** A compromised or failing hub can harm by **omission** — never advertising a new version. Because H1 keeps the signing key off the hub, the hub cannot forge a fresher bound, so the endpoint can detect the silence.

**What the invariants do not cover (review B8, unchanged).** No threat model names the adversaries each invariant stops (OPEN-5). B1 found its gaps by writing the adversaries down.

## 3. Decisions D42–D90 (consolidated register of 2026-09-02, every ID +7)

Text as written on 2026-09-02, with IDs renumbered. Vendor claims carry `[VERIFY]`; §10.1 gives the answer the review found for each. Where a later decision amends a row, the amending ID is noted in §5.


### 3.1 Knowledge base

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D42** | Hub connection grants KB access for answering before escalation. KB answering is a first-class deflection capability, not a secondary feature. Read-only, needs no privileged primitive, covers ticket classes the four Win32 primitives cannot reach. |
| **D43** | **Customer KB is never stored at rest on the endpoint.** Retrieval is hub-side. Reasons: query embedding needs the network anyway (D44); articles carry read criteria requiring per-user entitlement at query time; withdrawn articles must stop being served immediately, and sync protocols handle deletions badly. |
| **D44** | Query embedding requires a network call. **There is no offline dense retrieval path.** Titan Text Embeddings V2 is Bedrock-hosted `[VERIFY]`; dense retrieval requires query and corpus embedded by the same model, so a local substitute returns noise, not degraded results. Consequence of the existing embedding decision, not a new choice. |
| **D45** | **Generic KB ships inside the signed catalog bundle.** Vendor-authored, no entitlement rules, no customer data — safe at rest. Reuses an existing signed, versioned distribution channel; no new signing story; H1 unchanged. |
| **D46** | The generic bundle is pushed proactively, not fetched lazily. The moment it is needed is the moment the hub is unreachable. |
| **D47** | **Offline capability = tier-0 routing + lexical search over the generic bundle. No model, no customer content.** Converges with what §7 already specifies for standalone mode. |

**Rejected and recorded dead:** hub embeds query, endpoint searches a local index. Needs the network
anyway, so delivers no offline capability while still placing the corpus on disk with the
entitlement and retraction problems intact.

### 3.2 Connectivity

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D48** | **No user-facing mode toggle.** Mode is machine state, broker-owned, written at enrollment, changed only by authenticated enroll/unenroll. UI displays mode and cannot set it. A user-operable toggle is a security downgrade triggered by a button press. It also has nothing to toggle *to* — H3 means a joined endpoint holds no local credential. |
| **D48a** | The unenroll path is security-relevant and must be authenticated, or the downgrade is relocated rather than closed. |
| **D49** | **Three connectivity states:** joined-and-reachable, joined-and-unreachable, standalone. Surfaced distinctly — a user shown a generic "offline" for both will escalate about the wrong thing. |
| **D50** | Connectivity is determined by an **authenticated heartbeat** that also carries policy and catalog version. Not a reachability check: captive portals return 200 to anything, and a TCP connect proves nothing about credential validity. Collapses three signals that can otherwise disagree into one. |
| **D51** | Hysteresis: enter offline after N consecutive failures, leave only on a successful authenticated call. Mid-session capability loss is stated to the user rather than silently degrading. |

### 3.3 Hub inference

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D52** | In joined mode the endpoint uses the hub inference proxy, with device identity as the credential. |
| **D53** | **Single combined call.** Endpoint sends conversation state and user turn; hub retrieves, assembles, calls the model, returns a **structured proposal**. Retrieved chunks never leave the hub. The two-call alternative moves customer KB content hub → endpoint → hub and lands it in endpoint memory. |
| **D53a** | **Redaction stays on the endpoint**, before the hub call. §9 requires redaction before egress regardless of destination; endpoint → hub is egress. Hub-side redaction means the text already left the machine and the rule is decorative. |
| **D53b** | **Tier-0 routing stays on the endpoint** with no hub dependency. Preserves the network-outage case — where the agent is most valuable and the hub least reachable. |
| **D53c** | Haiku→Sonnet escalation policy and per-tenant metering/caps move to the hub. Escalation is a cost decision; `modelId` is already tenant config. Neither is possible in standalone. |

**Boundary clarification.** D53 does not violate "the hub must not own the conversation loop"
*provided* the invariant reads as **model proposes / endpoint validates / broker executes**. The
endpoint still validates every proposal against the signed catalog, still owns consent wording
(H3e), still owns execution. Where prompt concatenation happens is not a security boundary. See C1.

### 3.4 User schema

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D54** | Introduce a **user identity** entity distinct from device identity. D43 entitlement and D68 ticket history both require it. |
| **D55** | Model on **SCIM 2.0 core + enterprise extension**, not ServiceNow `sys_user`. The `sys_user` field set maps near one-to-one onto SCIM `[VERIFY]` — not coincidence, since enterprises typically drive `sys_user` from AD/Entra, making it a derived copy rather than a source of truth. SCIM buys ServiceNow compatibility plus Okta, Entra and Jira. Same reasoning as §12's refusal to depend on a vendor's tool-calling dialect. |
| **D56** | **`sys_id` is not the primary key** — it is one entry in an external-correlation map alongside Jira account ID, Entra object ID. §2 says most customers lack ServiceNow; a `sys_id` PK gives them all a null key. |
| **D57** | Retain `manager` explicitly. Required for approval routing on R2+ actions and for access/license request categories — the ticket classes the four primitives cannot touch. |
| **D58** | The user table is PII (email, employee number, manager chain, location) and is in scope for §9 retention and residency. Schema-per-tenant makes per-customer deletion tractable. |

### 3.5 Customer-hosted deployment

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D59** | Customer-local hub deployment remains supported; §5's dependency discipline is what preserves it. |
| **D59a** | **pgvector is a stated deployment requirement** on customer-supplied Postgres. D43 puts the vector index on the hub and §5 permits only Postgres and object storage. A version-and-extension constraint on hardware we do not control — surfaces on install day if undocumented. |
| **D59b** | Infrastructure cost falls in a local deployment; **inference cost does not.** The only genuinely low-cost configuration is customer-hosted hub *plus* customer-hosted OpenAI-compatible endpoint (§12 tier 3) — which is best-effort and excluded from deflection commitments. So the cheapest, most privacy-attractive configuration is the one we cannot make quality promises about. Recorded as a commercial position, not a defect. |
| **D59c** | A **minimum telemetry set is a condition of support** for customer-hosted deployments. Otherwise: no deflection measurement, no debugging, and canary rings mean nothing across a fleet we do not operate. Retrofitting onto an installed base is unpleasant. |

### 3.6 Session model

*(Recovered — these were discussed and assigned D60–D65 in conversation, then that range was reused
for device auth. Renumbered here. All ServiceNow claims unverified.)*

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D75** | A **session is a first-class entity**, distinct from any ticket. A session may produce zero, one, or several tickets. Modelled as session ↔ task with a many-to-many link. |
| **D76** | **Adopt the shape, not the schema.** ServiceNow's Interaction Management is believed to model exactly this — `interaction` plus `interaction_related_record` `[VERIFY]`. Field names remain ours; the ServiceNow adapter maps onto their tables, the Jira adapter maps the task half and keeps sessions entirely our side. **Note the contrast with D55:** there is no SCIM for tickets — Jira, ServiceNow and Zendesk disagree and no interchange standard has won — so the argument against modelling on a vendor schema applies to users but not to tickets. Borrowing the abstraction is still correct; borrowing the schema is not. |
| **D77** | Model the **base `task` fields**, not incident-specific ones `[VERIFY]`. Their inheritance model indicates which fields are universal; category, subcategory and `caller_id` are incident-flavoured and belong in the adapter. |
| **D78** | **`opened_by` is not `caller_id`** `[VERIFY]`. Filing on a user's behalf means `opened_by` is the integration account and `caller_id` is the affected user. Getting this wrong breaks their reporting, assignment rules, and any SLA keyed to the caller. Second place where D54/D55 user identity becomes load-bearing — we must resolve our user to their user reference. |
| **D79** | `assignment_group` is tenant configuration, never inferred. |
| **D79a** | **"ServiceNow adapter" is at minimum two record types.** Access, license and software-install requests are catalog requests with a different workflow and approval chain `[VERIFY: sc_req_item under sc_request]`, not incidents. §14 item 5 is scoped as one adapter and is not. |
| **D79b** | Session records are **ours by default**; mirroring sessions into the customer's ITSM is a per-tenant opt-in that changes the consent wording. Mirroring means every conversation leaves the endpoint, not just escalated ones — a categorically different privacy posture from "we file a ticket when we cannot fix it," and it collides with §8's instruction to give egress its own risk tier and consent wording. |
| **D79c** | §8's correlation requirement (audit carries ticket ID, ticket carries audit IDs) **is the session↔task relationship drawn by hand.** Adopting D75 replaces a bespoke correlation scheme with a modelled relationship. |

### 3.7 Device authentication

The space splits into two questions usually collapsed: **what credential the device holds**, and
**how it is presented on the wire**. Deciding "mTLS" answers only the second and silently commits to
operating a CA.

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D60** | Device identity is an **asymmetric keypair generated at enrollment by the broker**, TPM-backed and non-exportable where TPM 2.0 is present. Public key binds to a hub device record. Bearer tokens as steady-state identity are excluded for the reason H3 exists. |
| **D61** | Authentication is a **signed assertion exchanged for a short-lived access token**, not per-request signing. D53 makes every turn a hub call; TPM signing per-request is too slow `[VERIFY: order of tens of ms]`. Short expiry delivers revocation without CRL machinery. Pattern is OAuth 2.0 client credentials with private-key JWT client auth `[VERIFY: RFC 7523]`. |
| **D62** | **mTLS is rejected.** Three reasons, any one sufficient: (a) corporate TLS inspection terminates the connection and cannot present a client certificate, requiring a per-customer exemption negotiated at every deployment `[VERIFY: prevalence — ask design partner directly]`; (b) customer-hosted hubs would need our trust store or their own CA; (c) §5's "every extra daemon is a pager" applies harder to PKI than to Redis — certificate expiry is a fleet-wide outage with a date on it. |
| **D62a** | **Consequence:** ALB mutual-TLS is removed from the verification register. Device auth is decoupled from AWS ingress and behaves identically hosted or self-hosted. |
| **D63** | **No-TPM fallback:** software key under DPAPI machine scope in broker-owned ProgramData. The hub records key-protection level as device state, because weak-path devices may be capped at a lower risk tier. |
| **D64** | The device key lives **broker-side**. The agent requests tokens over the existing named pipe and never sees the key. Pipe is ACL'd and the calling binary's signature checked. Blast radius is bounded — a token grants what the agent already has — but the check is cheap. |
| **D65** | Same abstraction maps to macOS Secure Enclave. v1 Windows work is not throwaway. |

### 3.8 Enrollment

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D66** | **An enrollment secret buys a pending record, not fleet membership.** A tenant-wide secret in the MSI is the credential most likely to leak; under this model a leak costs a spam queue, not a fleet breach. |
| **D67** | **Enrollment is the mechanism for standalone → joined conversion** (§7 requirement 1) and writes the broker-owned mode state (D48). Unenrollment is the only path back and is authenticated. |
| **D67a** | TPM attestation at enrollment deferred to v2 hardening. |
| **D80** | **Auto-approve is the default, with a per-tenant toggle to IT-approval-only.** Auto-approve still writes the pending record and then approves it — one code path, policy flag on the end. The queue becomes a permanent enrollment audit log rather than a mode. |
| **D81** | **Under D80 the enrollment secret is the security boundary**, so it must carry that weight: per-tenant, rotatable, time-boxed. An enrollment secret with no expiry is a permanent skeleton key. |
| **D82** | **The secret is not baked into the MSI.** Delivered as install-time configuration, so the installer binary is not itself a credential. Installers get emailed, dropped on shares, and posted to vendor portals. |
| **D83** | **Rate limiting and anomaly detection on the enrollment endpoint**, with automatic fallback to queued approval on anomaly. This is the safety net that costs IT nothing when nothing is wrong. |
| **D83a** | **Bulk revocation by enrollment batch** in the console. Under D61 revocation is a row delete and tokens expire on their own — make sure the console can act at batch granularity. |

### 3.9 Ticket history

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D68** | **Own-history retrieval is a live read-through query**, filtered to the requesting user, against the ITSM (integrated) or internal store (self-contained). No ingestion, no embedding, no corpus. Ticket data is high-volume, constantly changing, customer-owned and PII-dense; replicating it would put PII in a vector index and pull §9 retention across the whole corpus. Depends on OPEN-1. |
| **D69** | **Status readback uses customer-visible comments only. Internal work notes are never surfaced to the end user.** `[VERIFY: believed comments vs work_notes]`. Whatever the field names, the distinction is real and the default must be visible-only. Reading internal engineer commentary aloud to the user it concerns is a serious and easily-shipped failure. |
| **D70** | **Cross-user historical similarity is deferred**, pending entitlement research. Requires a searchable corpus of all tickets — embedding PII, replicating customer data, and an entitlement model over records that may include HR cases, security incidents, terminations and executive escalations. A project with a privacy review attached, not a feature. |
| **D70a** | If taken up later, the likely safe form is **retrieval over resolved-and-sanitised tickets promoted into the KB**, not raw ticket records. Converts an entitlement problem into a curation problem, which draft-and-approve already handles. |

**Why the split matters.** D68 is days of work and materially improves diagnosis and status
answering. D70 sounds similar and is not. Committing to both at once puts the cheap, high-value
feature behind the expensive, high-risk one.

### 3.10 System of record

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D84** | **The hub is the system of record for tickets unless an ITSM integration exists.** Where one exists, the ITSM is SoR and the hub reads through. |
| **D85** | **The SoR flip must be supported** — a customer running self-contained for months then connecting ServiceNow. Must not lose history, must not require reinstall or reset. Structurally the same problem as §7's standalone → joined conversion and deserves the same treatment. Currently undefined; the first customer to do it will be an escalation. Open sub-questions: migrate, orphan, or dual-read; and what happens to reports spanning the flip. |
| **D86** | **Self-contained mode requires outbound email.** With the hub as SoR there is no assignment group, no on-call rotation, no L2 queue — those came free with the ITSM. Needed: notification to an IT alias, status updates to the requester, probably inbound reply handling. **This is a third dependency** and §5's "exactly two stateful dependencies" framing needs updating rather than quietly breaking. Deliverability, bounce handling and inbound parsing are real work, and it must also exist in customer-hosted deployments. |
| **D86a** | Binary SoR is likely too coarse for real orgs (ITSM for one department; incidents routed to ITSM while low-value requests stay local). **Not built in v1**, but the schema must not foreclose it. |

### 3.11 Dashboard, reporting and deflection

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D71** | **The dashboard's differentiated content is session and deflection analytics, not ticket analytics.** Customers with an ITSM already have ticket reporting and it is a large part of what they pay for; ours will be worse. What no other tool can produce is sessions that did not become tickets. |
| **D71a** | This is reinforced by D84: session analytics are **uniform across both deployments** because that data is always ours, whereas ticket analytics are degraded (read-through, rate-limited, subject to their field customizations) exactly where the customer already has better reporting. Build the uniform half first. |
| **D72** | Dimensions: by user, by category, by time, by outcome, by risk tier of actions taken. "By outcome" and "by risk tier" are ours alone and are what an IT lead wants during a rollout. |
| **D87** | **Two views: IT-lead operational and buyer/exec ROI.** |
| **D88** | The exec view is a **renewal artifact** — exportable and schedulable (monthly PDF or email), because executives do not log into vendor tools. |
| **D89** | **Three session outcomes: resolved (user explicitly confirmed), escalated (ticket created), unresolved (everything else, including abandonment). Only confirmed-resolved counts toward deflection.** A session where the user asked, got an answer and closed the window is indistinguishable in our data from one where they gave up and walked to IT. Counting abandonment as deflection produces a bigger number and loses the room the first time a competent IT lead challenges it. Every exec-view number drills down to underlying sessions. |
| **D90** | **The agent must request end-of-session confirmation.** This is a client behaviour requirement created by D89 and is not currently written down anywhere. |

**Defining deflection is a commercial decision, not a metrics one.** D89 produces a smaller headline
figure that survives scrutiny, which is worth more than a large one that does not.

### 3.12 Internal ticket store

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D73** | **Promoted from §4 priority 3 to core.** It is the system of record for every customer without an ITSM, and §2 states that is most of them. **Resolves C4.** |

**Scope warning.** §5 rejected Zammad and Chatwoot as dependencies for sound reasons. Promoting the
internal store does not remove the requirements those products exist to satisfy — it relocates them
into our codebase where we also own the bugs. A store an IT lead will accept needs assignment and
reassignment, SLA timers on business calendars, queues and routing, email ingest with threaded
reply, attachments, audit history, search, saved views, bulk operations, and a permissions model.

**Recommendation:** define an explicit v1 subset in writing and defend it, rather than discovering
the list feature by feature. Plausible pilot subset: create, assign, state machine, comments split
by visibility (D69), attachments, search, dashboard. Everything else is a deliberate later decision.

### 3.13 Terminology

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D74** | **"Standalone" is overloaded and must be split in all documents.** §7 uses it for the endpoint deployment mode (local API key, capped R1, no hub). Later usage means the product operating without ITSM integration. These are independent axes — a fully joined fleet with no ServiceNow is hub-connected and ITSM-less. **Proposed terms: endpoint mode = standalone / joined; ITSM posture = integrated / self-contained.** Used consistently throughout this document. |
